python


#!/usr/bin/env python3
import sys, json, time
import kaggle_environments as ke
def load_agent(path):
    src = open(path).read()
    ns = {}
    exec(compile(src, path, "exec"), ns)
    return ns["agent"]
def main():
    a_path, b_path = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    seeds = [int(x) for x in sys.argv[3].split(",")]
    swap = "swap" in sys.argv
    a = load_agent(a_path)  # separate namespace per file
    b = load_agent(b_path)
    ...
    for seed in seeds:
        env = ke.make("kaggriculture", configuration={"seed": seed}, debug=False)
        env.run([a, b])  # callables allowed?

    if pw2 <= 16 and money >= 2000 and day <= 26:
        n_ab = min(60, int((money - 1500) // max(1, pw2)))
        if n_ab > 0:
            idx = emit(["BUY_PRODUCT", "WHEAT", n_ab])
            arrive("shed", "WHEAT", n_ab, idx)
            money -= n_ab * pw2
            shed["WHEAT"] = shed.get("WHEAT", 0) + n_ab
Resell: when pw >= 24 and we have wheat above keep_w — the flood sell already dumps everything above keep_w each morning. So the round trip: buy at ≤16 (pushes price UP +1/unit — wait, buying pushes price up which is anti-war for wheat... but at ≤16 someone already crashed it below our flood floor; we capture the rebound). Cap 60 units, only when money ≥ 2000 (won't starve hires/seeds). Sell side automatic next morning via flood. Profit ~60×$8 = $480/cycle. Minor, but user-directed. Also the buy itself removes enemy's crash depth on wheat — raising wheat back toward $20-25 where our flood wants it anyway. Fine.

Change 7 — hires: keep v48.

python


#!/usr/bin/env python3
import sys, time, io, contextlib
import kaggle_environments as ke
def play(a, b, seed):
    env = ke.make("kaggriculture", configuration={"seed": seed}, debug=False)
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(io.StringIO()):
        env.run([a, b])
    s0, s1 = env.state[0], env.state[1]
    return (s0.reward, s1.reward, s0.status, s1.status)
def main():
    A, B = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    seeds = [int(x) for x in sys.argv[3].split(",")]
    swap = len(sys.argv) > 4 and sys.argv[4] == "swap"
    games = [(A, B)] + ([(B, A)] if swap else [])
    wins = {A: 0, B: 0}
    diffs = []
    for seed in seeds:
        for x, y in games:
            t0 = time.time()
            rx, ry, sx, sy = play(x, y, seed)
            tag = f"seed {seed}: {x.split('/')[-1]} vs {y.split('/')[-1]}"
            if sx != "DONE" or sy != "DONE":
                print(f"{tag}  STATUS {sx}/{sy}  r={rx}/{ry}")
            d = (rx or 0) - (ry or 0)
            diffs.append(d)
            w = x if d > 0 else (y if d < 0 else None)
            if w: wins[w] += 1
            print(f"{tag}  {rx} vs {ry}  diff {d:+.0f}  ({time.time()-t0:.0f}s)")
    print(f"\n=== {A.split('/')[-1]} vs {B.split('/')[-1]} ===")
    print(f"wins: {wins[A]} - {wins[B]}  (of {len(diffs)} games)")
    print(f"median diff (A-B): {sorted(diffs)[len(diffs)//2]:+.0f}   mean {sum(diffs)/len(diffs):+.0f}")
main()

war/h2h.py

h2h.py
PYTHON
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""H2H bench: python3 h2h.py A.py B.py seed1,seed2,... [swap]
Prints per-game golds + W/L + median diff oriented A-minus-B."""
import sys, time, io, contextlib
import kaggle_environments as ke

def play(a, b, seed):
    env = ke.make("kaggriculture", configuration={"seed": seed}, debug=False)
    buf = io.StringIO()
    with contextlib.redirect_stdout(buf):
        env.run([a, b])
    s0, s1 = env.state[0], env.state[1]
    return (s0.reward, s1.reward, s0.status, s1.status)

def main():
    A, B = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    seeds = [int(x) for x in sys.argv[3].split(",")]
    swap = len(sys.argv) > 4 and sys.argv[4] == "swap"
    games = [(A, B, 1)] + ([(B, A, -1)] if swap else [])
    wins = [0, 0]  # A wins, B wins
    diffs = []
    for seed in seeds:
        for x, y, sign in games:
            t0 = time.time()
            rx, ry, sx, sy = play(x, y, seed)
            nx, ny = x.split("/")[-1], y.split("/")[-1]
            d = sign * ((rx or 0) - (ry or 0))
            if sx != "DONE" or sy != "DONE":
                print(f"seed {seed} {nx} vs {ny}: STATUS {sx}/{sy} r={rx}/{ry}")
                print(buf.getvalue()[-500:])
            if d > 0: wins[0] += 1
            elif d < 0: wins[1] += 1
            diffs.append(d)
            print(f"seed {seed} {nx:16s} vs {ny:16s} {rx:>8.0f} - {ry:>8.0f}  diff {d:+7.0f}  ({time.time()-t0:.0f}s)")
    na, nb = A.split("/")[-1], B.split("/")[-1]
    med = sorted(diffs)[len(diffs)//2]
    print(f"\n=== {na} vs {nb}: {wins[0]}-{wins[1]} (of {len(diffs)}) median diff {med:+.0f} mean {sum(diffs)/len(diffs):+.0f} ===")

main()

TT's agent is clean and wrappable:

v52 design (TT engine + war overlay):

python


import tt_router as tt  (embedded — for submission we'd need to inline both; for now wrap by importing... but for the ladder it must be ONE file. For benching, import works if both files present. For submission, merge the files: paste tt_router.py contents + wrapper.)
def agent(obs, config=None):
    base = tt.agent(obs, config)   # perfect routes + its market plan
    p = int(obs["player"]); day = obs.get("day",0); hour = obs.get("hour",0)
    # WAR OVERLAY on market orders only:
    mkt = [list(o) for o in base["market"]]
    # 1) endgame full dump: d28+ replace all orders with sells of everything (incl wheat/fert)
    # 2) append war sells for products when there's room (orders <= 10): mega-sells for items not already being sold
    # 3) spy-gated wheat flood: if enemy wheat-heavy, sell wheat beyond feed reserve
    ...
    return {"farmer": base["farmer"], "hands": base["hands"], "market": mkt[:10]}
Key details:

TT's tapes already include market orders (buys/sells tuned to its plan). We must not break its HIRE/BUY_SEED/BUY_ANIMAL/BUY_LAND flow (those fund the engine). War sells should be ADDED in empty order slots or replace existing SELLs.
TT's own sells: from the d20 h0 sample, its market = [BUY_SEED WHEAT 9, HIRE ×9] — no sells at h0! Its sells happen at h20-23 (the mega-orders). So morning slots are mostly free for our war sells!
Danger: our mega-sells at h0 could eat slots needed for hires (10-cap). E.g. d20 h0 has 10 orders already (1 seed + 9 hires). No room. So war sells must be smart: only append when slots free, or put war sells in hours where the tape has few orders.
Simplest robust rule:

Drop TT's own SELL orders, replace with our war sells (mega for products; wheat = shed - keep_w; fert = shed - small keep; d29 dump everything).

Safe layout: at each hour, orders = TT's non-SELL orders (hires/buys/land) + war sells filling remaining slots up to 10. TT's sells (h20-23 mega) get REPLACED by our war sells (which are also mega) — same effect plus ours run every hour there's room.
keep_w: TT's feed plan — its tapes include BUY_PRODUCT WHEAT and PICKUP WHEAT 4-5 (feeding). If we sell all its wheat at h0, its feeding breaks? Feeding uses unit inventory wheat (PICKUP from shed → FEED). If we dump the shed wheat, the tape's PICKUP WHEAT fails → animals unfed → escapes! CRITICAL: keep enough wheat in shed for the tape's feeding. TT picks up 4-5 wheat/day at h0-ish. Keep reserve = n_an + 6? Animals ~17 → keep 20. Or: don't wheat-flood TT's engine at all unless enemy wheat-heavy (spy gate) — and even then keep n_an + 8.
FERT: tapes sell FERTILIZER 1/day and collect fert for melon/strb. Keep a small fert reserve (5).
Plan: v52 = wrapper with:

replaces TT sells with war sells in leftover slots,
wheat: keep_w = enemy-gated flood with reserve n_an*2,
endgame d28+: dump all incl. wheat/fert (feed still needed d28? animals produce d29 — keep n_an wheat through d28, dump at d29),
fert: sell shed beyond 6.
Slots: at h0 (10 orders), if tape has 10 non-sell orders → no war sells that hour (fine, next hour). Hours 1-19 usually have 0-3 orders → war sells fire there. That maintains all-day selling pressure — even better than TT's own evening-only.

"""v52: TT-router engine (taped elite routes) + war seller overlay.
Bench build: imports tt_router.py from the same directory.
The overlay replaces the tape's SELL orders with war sells (first-seller,
full dump, spy-gated wheat flood, endgame horn dump) while preserving every
non-SELL order (hires/seeds/animals/land) — those fund the engine. The
tape's unit actions are untouched. Feed reserve protects the tape's
PICKUP-WHEAT feeding loop.
"""
import os as _os
import sys as _sys
_sys.path.insert(0, _os.path.dirname(_os.path.abspath(__file__)))
import tt_router as _tt

WAR_ITEMS = ("MELON", "STRAWBERRY", "MILK", "WOOL", "EGG", "CARROT", "TOMATO")

def agent(obs, config=None):
    base = _tt.agent(obs, config)
    p = int(obs["player"])
    day = int(obs.get("day", 0))
    farms = obs.get("farms", [])
    priv = obs.get("private", {}) or {}
    shed = priv.get("shed", {}) or {}

    # ---- spy: enemy wheat commitment ----
    my_w = en_w = en_an = 0
    n_an = 0
    for i, fm in enumerate(farms):
        if not isinstance(fm, dict):
            continue
        w = a = 0
        for row in fm.get("tiles", []):
            for t in row:
                if isinstance(t, dict):
                    if t.get("kind") == "PLANT" and t.get("crop") == "WHEAT":
                        w += 1
                    elif "animal" in t:
                        a += 1
        if i == p:
            my_w, n_an = w, a
        else:
            en_w, en_an = w, a

    keep_w = 0
    if day < 28:
        keep_w = max(6, n_an * 2)          # tape feeds from the shed daily
    else:
        keep_w = n_an if day < 29 else 0   # d28 feed still buys d29 output

    orders = []
    for o in base.get("market", []):
        if isinstance(o, list) and o and o[0] == "SELL":
            continue                        # replace tape sells with war sells
        orders.append(list(o))
        if len(orders) >= 10:
            break

    def room():
        return 10 - len(orders)

    # war sells fill the remaining slots (tape's hires/buys keep priority —
    # orders beyond 10 are dropped by the engine, and hires fund everything)
    if day >= 29:
        # horn: dump absolutely everything
        for it in ("MELON", "STRAWBERRY", "MILK", "WOOL", "EGG", "CARROT",
                   "TOMATO", "FERTILIZER", "WHEAT"):
            if room() <= 0:
                break
            if it == "WHEAT":
                n = shed.get(it, 0)
            else:
                n = 10 ** 6
            if n > 0:
                orders.append(["SELL", it, n])
    else:
        for it in WAR_ITEMS:
            if room() <= 0:
                break
            orders.append(["SELL", it, 10 ** 6])   # mega: drains shed, aborts empty
        fert_keep = 6
        if shed.get("FERTILIZER", 0) > fert_keep and room() > 0:
            orders.append(["SELL", "FERTILIZER", shed["FERTILIZER"] - fert_keep])
        # wheat flood only vs wheat-heavy enemies; never touch the feed reserve
        if en_w >= 10 and en_w >= 1.2 * max(1, my_w) and room() > 0:
            n = shed.get("WHEAT", 0) - keep_w
            if n > 0:
                orders.append(["SELL", "WHEAT", n])

    return {"farmer": base.get("farmer", ["PASS"]),
            "hands": base.get("hands", []),
            "market": orders[:10]}

Configuration
# Run this command:
gemini mcp add --transport http kaggle https://www.kaggle.com/mcp

# Or add this to your ~/.gemini/settings.json file:
{
    "mcpServers": {
        "kaggle": {
            "httpUrl": "https://www.kaggle.com/mcp"
        }
    }
}
Authentication
# Run this command:
/mcp auth kaggle

# Or, for token authentication:
{
    "mcpServers": {
        "kaggle": {
            "transport": "http",
            "httpUrl": "https://www.kaggle.com/mcp",
            "headers": {
                "Authorization": "Bearer <YOUR_TOKEN>"
            }
        }
    },
}
    
# If you don't already have a token, go to Settings > Generate New Token > Copy.
Usage
# To upload a submission, prompt your client to use the
# "mcp_kaggle_start_competition_submission_upload" tool. 
# Then, use "kaggle_mcp_submit_to_competition" to submit it to the competition.



Cancel

Now I can see the router's full design and its failure modes. Critical findings:

1. Planting: if h >= 22: continue — plants only get done before h22, fine. But the bigger issue: plant_jobs only use seeds_avail(crop, 5) — seeds in shed at h5. And plant crops ordered CARROT → STRB → MELON → TOMATO → WHEAT with each seed = one job on tiles sorted NEAR SHED FIRST. Empty tiles FAR from shed never get planted if seeds run out (each seed = 1 job; seeds_avail limits). That plus wheat seeds being consumed for feed... The empty tiles (12-39 from d10) are the FAR tiles — the wedge nearest-first planting never reaches them because plant jobs pick spots[0] = nearest to shed — wait, spots[0] is nearest-to-shed of REMAINING empties, so as near ones fill, jobs walk outward... unless seeds run out. With targets buying limited seeds... AND: only ONE pass at h5 seed availability — if seeds arrive later (bought at h1-4 with arrival h2-5...), or PLANT tasks get claimed but the claimer is busy walking, by the time it's free the hour >= 22 block kills it. Also multiple units claim DIFFERENT plant tasks and all walk far...

2. The cow at (3,4) never harvested — now I see why! Animal server routes: n_servers = max(2, min(4, (len(ani)+2)//3)) — 2-4 server units handle ALL animals, and each server's route is PICKUP wheat → FEED/CARE/COLLECT/HARVESTA for its animals in ani_sorted order (sorted by x+y!). The route is executed SEQUENTIALLY — one task per hour, walking animal to animal. With ~17 animals split among 3-4 servers, each server has 4-6 animals × 3-4 tasks each = 12-24 tasks + walking = THE WHOLE DAY. If the route doesn't finish by h23, the tail tasks (HARVESTA!) never run — and next morning the route REBUILDS the same way (FEED first, ...), so the tail is starved EVERY DAY. The cow at (3,4): ani_sorted sorts by (x+y, x) → (3,4) sum=7... geese at low sums go first; (3,4) cow is mid-list... but 51 hours at cap means its HARVESTA was in the never-reached tail repeatedly.

Also — the route has FEED, CARE, COLLECT, HARVESTA per animal — 4 tasks per animal per day is insane; COLLECT (fertilizer) daily for every animal plus CARE daily... The servers drown.

3. PASS hours: the wedge units finish their wedge by early afternoon, then hit the backlog scan; if backlog is empty/claimed by others, they PASS. With plant jobs limited by seeds and DIG capped at 14, afternoons = idle. Plus the "no cands → walk to centroid" doesn't exist in this lineage (that was v48). Wedge units that finish → backlog → PASS.

The fix set (v56) — surgical, high-leverage:

F1. Split animal serving properly + harvest-first: reorder server routes so HARVESTA comes BEFORE CARE/COLLECT for each animal (milk at cap = active production loss; care/collect are bonuses). Even better: put HARVESTA tasks at the FRONT of the server route (after pickup). Simple change in the route build: emit HARVESTA first per animal.

F2. Spread animals across MORE servers: n_servers = max(2, min(4, ...)) → use up to 6 servers when animals ≥ 12 (we run 17). With 4 tasks/animal and 24h, 3 animals/server finishes; 6 animals/server can't.

F3. Plant EVERY empty tile daily: plant_jobs currently limited by seeds_avail(crop,5) — one seed per job. Keep that, but ADD: after seeds-based jobs, if empties remain and WHEAT seeds available beyond... actually the real constraint is seeds bought at h0 (targets). The morning buy targets: wheat 32ish at late game → 32 seeds → 32 plant jobs → covers most. But empty 22-39 persist → seeds ARE the constraint (money gates at h0 buy). Increase: allow a SECOND seed buy pass at h1 with leftover money?? The plan_market already buys what targets say with money floor 300-400. Hmm — but empty tiles persisting means targets are met per the BOOK (tile counts include standing plants) while empties exist?? The gap math: targets = tile counts; gap = target - counts - seeds; if counts (standing) = target, no buy... but empties exist because tiles FREED by harvests during YESTERDAY aren't counted in the morning book (counts computed from morning state — tiles harvested yesterday are empty and counted... _remaining fraction-weighted...). The empty tiles might be far ones where plant jobs DID exist but h>=22 or claim starvation killed them._

Pragmatic fix for empties: (a) raise DIG cap 14→30, (b) plant jobs: allow ANY hour (drop h>=22 to h>=23 — planting at h22 still allows water at h23!), (c) sort plant jobs NEAR→FAR per unit claim... the backlog claim already gives first-claimable to whoever's free (nearest unit takes next job in list order = near-shed first — far tiles claimed last by whoever's free, walking far).

F4. Reduce PASS: idle units adopt unfinished plant/DIG backlog — the claim system already does this; the issue is jobs RUN OUT. With F3's bigger job list, idles absorb work.

F5. COLLECT every-other-day: fertilizer collect is 1/animal/day; collecting from 17 animals daily = 17 tasks. Alternate: COLLECT only on even days or when fert stock < 20. Cuts the server routes by ~40%. CARE similar (care only boosts yield bonus) — care daily is fine for servers with few animals.

Math (from engine + our routes):

Animal ops per day: FEED (1 op at animal tile), CARE (1), COLLECT_FERTILIZER (1), HARVEST (1 when ready). Each op = 1 hour ON TILE + walk time between animals.
Walk cost: animals clustered (structures near shed) — say 1-2 steps between adjacent animals, ~0-2 from shed.
Per animal per day: 4 ops + ~3-4 walk steps ≈ 7-8 unit-hours... but realistically: FEED+CARE+COLLECT daily = 3 ops + walks; HARVEST on production days only (cow every 2d, sheep every 3d, goose daily).
One unit works ~23 hours (h0 farmer passive 1). Effective ~20 after walks.
So one hire manages: 20 / (3 ops + ~1.5 walk avg + 0.5 harvest avg) ≈ 20/5 ≈ 4 animals/day. With 20 animals → 5 servers. We ran 2-4 servers (overloaded!) — that's why tails starved.

Thought for 27 seconds

Now I understand v58's assignment architecture:

Animal servers: units sorted by distance to animals, top n_servers get a pre-planned route: PICKUP wheat at shed → for each assigned animal (sorted by position, chunked contiguously): HARVESTA/FEED/CARE/COLLECT.

Field wedges: field tasks sorted by ANGLE around the centroid (a radial "pie" split), then split into K contiguous wedges, one per available unit. Within a wedge, nearest-neighbor greedy ordering from spawn. This is the angular-wedge design.

Backlog: BUILD/PLACE/planting/weeding handled in the hour loop (claims).

So the "football play" already exists in embryonic form: wedges are zones split by angle. The problem the user identifies: wedges are re-computed EVERY DAY from the angular sort — a hand's wedge on day 12 may be totally different from day 11, and wedge composition shifts as tasks come and go (watering in the morning, harvesting later). Units criss-cross. There's no STABLE zone ownership.

The zone-lock improvement (v62):

Stable quadrant-based zones: each non-server unit OWNS a quadrant (or half-quad if many hands). Zone membership is fixed by unit index + day structure (not re-sorted by angle each day).
Tasks within a zone go ONLY to the zone's owner(s); overflow (zone has no tasks, or owner is busy) spills to nearest tasks outside.
Servers own the animal cluster (already stable-ish since animals are clustered from v58's cluster builds).
The current wedge split by angle around centroid is actually reasonably stable IF the task distribution is stable... but tasks change composition daily (watering vs harvesting vs digging). Also avail excludes servers, so when crew size changes (math crew formula!), wedges re-slice completely. With v58's math-driven crew, want varies 8-14 → wedge count changes daily → everyone's zone shifts. Zone-lock by QUADRANT is stable: quads are fixed geography.

Let me write the patch. The anchor is the whole field-wedge block:

python


    # ---- field wedges (water / harvest / fertilize) ----
    field = [t for t in tasks if t[0] in ("WATER", "HARVESTC", "FERTILIZE", "DIGP")]
    if field:
        cx = sum(t[1][0] for t in field) / len(field)
        cy = sum(t[1][1] for t in field) / len(field)
        order = sorted(field, key=lambda t: math.atan2(t[1][1] - cy, t[1][0] - cx))
        avail = [i for i in range(1, n_units) if i not in (servers if ani else [])]
        if not avail:
            avail = list(range(1, n_units))
        K = len(avail)
        n_f = len(order)
        for j, i in enumerate(avail):
            wedge = order[j * n_f // K:(j + 1) * n_f // K]
            if not wedge:
                continue
            if any(t[0] == "FERTILIZE" for t in wedge):
                routes[i].append(("PICKUP", _shed_near(spawn[i] or (4, 4)), "FERTILIZER",
                                  min(6, sum(1 for t in wedge if t[0] == "FERTILIZE"))))
            pos = spawn[i] or (4, 4)
            rem = list(wedge)
            while rem:
                ni = min(range(len(rem)), key=lambda k: _d(pos, rem[k][1]))
                t = rem.pop(ni)
                routes[i].append((t[0], t[1], t[2]))
                pos = t[1]
Replace with quadrant-zone version:

python


    # ---- field ZONES (football play): each hand owns a QUADRANT — stable
    # geography, day over day; load-balanced within; idle hands cover the
    # heaviest zone. Replaces angular wedges that re-sliced every day. ----
    field = [t for t in tasks if t[0] in ("WATER", "HARVESTC", "FERTILIZE", "DIGP")]
    if field:
        avail = [i for i in range(1, n_units) if i not in (servers if ani else [])]
        if not avail:
            avail = list(range(1, n_units))
        def zone_of(p):
            return ("N" if p[1] < 5 else "S") + ("W" if p[0] < 5 else "E")
        zones = {}
        for t in field:
            zones.setdefault(zone_of(t[1]), []).append(t)
        znames = sorted(zones, key=lambda z: -len(zones[z]))   # heaviest first
        K = len(avail)
        # proportional hand allocation (largest remainder), min 1 per zone
        alloc = {z: 0 for z in znames}
        for z in znames:
            alloc[z] = K * len(zones[z]) // max(1, len(field))
        used = sum(alloc.values())
        for z in sorted(znames, key=lambda z: -(K * len(zones[z]) / max(1, len(field)) - alloc[z])):
            if used >= K:
                break
            if alloc[z] < len(zones[z]):     # don't over-staff tiny zones
                alloc[z] += 1
                used += 1
        while used > K:                      # trim from lightest zones
            z = min(znames, key=lambda z: (alloc[z] <= 1, len(zones[z])))
            if alloc[z] <= 1:
                break
            alloc[z] -= 1
            used -= 1
        # hands -> zones: stable (index order -> heaviest zones first)
        hand_zone = {}
        zi = 0
        for i in avail:
            while zi < len(znames) and (alloc[znames[zi]] <= 0):
                zi += 1
            if zi >= len(znames):
                break
            hand_zone[i] = znames[zi]
            alloc[znames[zi]] -= 1
        # idle hands (no zone left) cover the heaviest zone's tail
        for i in avail:
            if i not in hand_zone:
                hand_zone[i] = znames[0]
        # build routes: within a zone, split by angle then nearest-neighbor
        zone_hands = {}
        for i, z in hand_zone.items():
            zone_hands.setdefault(z, []).append(i)
        for z, hands in zone_hands.items():
            ts = zones.get(z, [])
            if not ts:
                continue
            cx = sum(t[1][0] for t in ts) / len(ts)
            cy = sum(t[1][1] for t in ts) / len(ts)
            ts_sorted = sorted(ts, key=lambda t: math.atan2(t[1][1] - cy, t[1][0] - cx))
            n_t = len(ts_sorted)
            for j, i in enumerate(hands):
                wedge = ts_sorted[j * n_t // len(hands):(j + 1) * n_t // len(hands)]
                if not wedge:
                    continue
                if any(t[0] == "FERTILIZE" for t in wedge):
                    routes[i].append(("PICKUP", _shed_near(spawn[i] or (4, 4)), "FERTILIZER",
                                      min(6, sum(1 for t in wedge if t[0] == "FERTILIZE"))))
                pos = spawn[i] or (4, 4)
                rem = list(wedge)
                while rem:
                    ni = min(range(len(rem)), key=lambda k: _d(pos, rem[k][1]))
                    t = rem.pop(ni)
                    routes[i].append((t[0], t[1], t[2]))
                    pos = t[1]
Wait — one subtlety: zones.get(z, []) for hands assigned to a zone that got no tasks... alloc only assigns hands to zones in znames (zones WITH tasks) unless overflow. hand_zone default znames[0] for extras — znames[0] has tasks by definition. So ts never empty except... alloc min-1-per-zone loop: if alloc[z] < len(zones[z]) — a zone with 1 task gets max 1 hand. OK.

Also the servers set: servers if ani else [] — when no animals, avail = all. Fine.

Edge: K * len(zones[z]) // max(1, len(field)) — integer floor; then largest-remainder top-up. Trim when >K. OK.*

Also idle overflow hands: they join znames[0] (heaviest) — good ("cover the heaviest zone").

Within-zone angular split keeps sub-stability.

This is ONE structural change (wedge→zone). Bench: solo smoke + work/PASS diag, then H2H vs v58 (gate) and vs nb3 (gap).

THE COMPLETE ENGINE TRUTH — this is gold. Let me write out every rule that matters for the user's system:

PLANT RULES:

consecutive_unwatered >= 2 → tile becomes WEED (death). Watering resets to 0. So a crop must be watered at least every other day or it DIES. This is the user's "water every other day" — it's the SURVIVAL cadence! Watering every other day = crops stay alive but:
Non-ongoing (wheat/carrot/melon): yield comes ONLY from watering: +1/unit per watering in window [(max_yield_day+1)//2 .. max_yield_day] (+2 if fertilized that day). So skipping waterings in the window = skipping yield. Every-other-day watering of wheat = half yield. NO GOOD for non-ongoing.
Ongoing (tomato/strb): yield accumulates at EOD on days_since_first % interval == 0 days: +1 (+2 if watered AND fertilized that day — "Fertilizer bonus only applies on watered days"). KEY: ongoing crops get their +1 on production days EVEN IF NOT WATERED — watering only matters for (a) survival (every other day), (b) doubling on production days if fertilized. And production_count > max_yield stops accumulation.
STRAWBERRY: interval 2 → production every 2 days → water strb ONLY on production days = every other day = 100% of base yield + full fert bonus! The user's every-other-day watering is EXACTLY OPTIMAL for strb (the biggest crop) — survival cadence AND production cadence are the same days!
TOMATO: interval 1 → daily production → water daily for the fert bonus (or every other for survival, losing the doubling).
Decay: non-ongoing after max_lifespan: every 2 steps (hours!) yield −1. And strb: production_count == max_yield sets lifespan → dies after full production (4 productions = 8 days).
ANIMAL RULES (huge!):

consecutive_unfed >= 2 → ESCAPES (structure stays). So feed EVERY OTHER DAY is safe (survival) — feeding daily is NOT required to keep them! But:
Production: on days_since_first % interval == 0: +1 base + pending_care_bonus if fed today. CARE sets pending bonus only when BOTH cared AND fed that day. So the care bonus = extra yield units for +$ per care.
COW interval 2: produces every 2 days. Feed+care only on production days → every other day: gets base 1 + bonus 1 = 2 units per production with HALF the feed and HALF the ops! Skip feeding on off days (safe: consecutive_unfed hits 1, reset on feed day).
SHEEP interval 3: every 3 days — feed+care every 3rd day (production day)! consecutive_unfed would hit 2 → DEATH. Wait: unfed day1, unfed day2 → escapes BEFORE day3's feed. So sheep MUST be fed at least every other day even though production is every 3. Feed every other day, but care+feed on production days for the bonus.

python


for a in S["animals"]:
    an = ANIMALS[a["kind"]]
    dsf = (day + 1) - a["placed"] - an["first_yield_day"]
    prod = dsf >= 0 and dsf % an["interval"] == 0
    survival = a["unfed"] >= 1          # must feed today or escape tomorrow
    if not a["fed"] and (prod or survival):
        tasks.append(("FEED", a["pos"], "WHEAT"))
    if prod and not a["cared"]:
        tasks.append(("CARE", a["pos"], None))
    if not a["collected"]:
        tasks.append(("COLLECT", a["pos"], None))
    if a["yield"] >= (2 if a["kind"] == "GOOSE" else 3):
        tasks.append(("HARVESTA", a["pos"], None))

New code:

python


    # ---- QUAD-BANDED SERPENTINE ROUTES (the football play): every unit is
    # assigned to a quadrant; within the quad, row-bands split across its
    # hands; each hand sweeps its rows in serpentine order servicing crops
    # AND the animal line in one pass. No dedicated servers, no dead space. ----
    routes = {i: [] for i in range(n_units)}
    def _quad_of(p):
        return ("N" if p[1] < 5 else "S") + ("W" if p[0] < 5 else "E")
    owned_q = sorted(S["quadrants"]) or ["NW"]
    # task pools per quad
    qpool = {q: {} for q in owned_q}          # row -> [(serp_idx, (kind,pos,arg))]
    def _add(task):
        kind, pos, arg = task
        q = _quad_of(pos)
        if q not in qpool:
            qpool[q] = {}
        y0 = 0 if q[0] == "N" else 5
        x0 = 0 if q[1] == "W" else 5
        r = pos[1] - y0
        idx = (pos[0] - x0) if r % 2 == 0 else (4 - (pos[0] - x0))
        qpool[q].setdefault(r, []).append((idx, (kind, pos, arg)))
    for t in tasks:
        _add(t)
    # units per quad: 3 base per quad + extras to heaviest; farmer included
    nwork = n_units
    load = {q: sum(len(v) for v in qpool.get(q, {}).values()) for q in owned_q}
    assign = {}
    counts = {q: 0 for q in owned_q}
    for i in range(nwork):
        # prefer heaviest quad with fewest hands (min hands first: 3/quad)
        q = min(owned_q, key=lambda qq: (counts[qq] >= 3, counts[qq] / (1 + load[qq]), owned_q.index(qq)))
        assign[i] = q
        counts[q] += 1
    # build each unit's route: its quad's rows split into contiguous bands
    for i in range(nwork):
        q = assign[i]
        rows = sorted(qpool.get(q, {}).keys())
        mates = [j for j in range(nwork) if assign[j] == q]
        k = max(1, len(mates))
        # contiguous row bands
        my_bi = mates.index(i)
        bands = [rows[b * len(rows) // k:(b + 1) * len(rows) // k] for b in range(k)]
        my_rows = bands[my_bi] if my_bi < len(bands) else []
        for r in sorted(my_rows):
            for _, (kind, pos, arg) in sorted(qpool[q].get(r, [])):
                routes[i].append((kind, pos, arg))

New: per-quad quota lines:

python


    # ANIMAL LINES (user spec): 5 per quad in a straight line on the quad's
    # shed-side row — one worker covers the line in a single pass.
    # Quad quotas for the 6/6/8 herd:
    QUOTA = {"NW": {"COW": 2, "SHEEP": 1, "GOOSE": 2},
             "NE": {"COW": 2, "SHEEP": 2, "GOOSE": 1},
             "SW": {"COW": 1, "SHEEP": 2, "GOOSE": 2},
             "SE": {"COW": 1, "SHEEP": 1, "GOOSE": 3}}
    def _line_cells(q):
        y = 3 if q[0] == "N" else 6          # shed-side row of the quad
        x0 = 0 if q[1] == "W" else 5
        return [(x, y) for x in range(x0, x0 + 5)]
    # current per-quad animal counts by kind (standing animals)
    q_have = {q: {"COW": 0, "SHEEP": 0, "GOOSE": 0} for q in QUOTA}
    for a in S["animals"]:
        q = ("N" if a["pos"][1] < 5 else "S") + ("W" if a["pos"][0] < 5 else "E")
        if q in q_have:
            q_have[q][a["kind"]] += 1
    # + animals bought today (bought_kinds) and shed stock — allocate to quota
    alloc = [list(bought_kinds)]  # kinds needing a home today
    # include shed-stocked animals for existing empty structures handled below
    reserved = set()
    def _place_kind(kind):
        struct = ANIMALS[kind]["structure"]
        best_q, best_c = None, None
        for q in sorted(QUOTA):
            if q not in S["quadrants"]:
                continue
            if q_have[q][kind] >= QUOTA[q][kind]:
                continue
            for c in _line_cells(q):
                if c in reserved or c in SHED_TILES:
                    continue
                tile_free = c in sim["empty_at"] or c in sim["struct_at"]
                struct_ok = c in sim["struct_at"] and sim["struct_at"][c] == struct and c not in sim["animal_at"]
                if struct_ok:
                    best_q, best_c = q, c
                    break
                if tile_free and c in sim["empty_at"]:
                    best_q, best_c = q, c   # build here (first free line cell)
                    break
            if best_c:
                break
        if not best_c:
            return False
        reserved.add(best_c)
        if best_c in sim["struct_at"]:
            backlog.append(["PLACE", best_c, kind, -1])
        else:
            backlog.append(["BUILD", best_c, struct, -1])
            backlog.append(["PLACE", best_c, kind, -1])
        q_have[best_q][kind] += 1
        return True
    for kind in bought_kinds:
        _place_kind(kind)

    def _place_kind(kind):
        struct = ANIMALS[kind]["structure"]
        free_cell = None
        for q in sorted(QUOTA):
            if q not in S["quadrants"] or q_have[q][kind] >= QUOTA[q][kind]:

            return True
        return False
